4:T6c8,The method is built around cyclic depth folding. The Transformer is partitioned into P block groups, with one group placed on each worker. A batch enters the system at its local worker, and that worker index defines the cyclic starting point for that data stream. Hidden states then circulate through the block groups in a worker-indexed cyclic order for a chosen number of loop passes. As a result, the same block group appears at different source-relative depths depending on where the sequence entered the cycle. The paper calls this depth-role superposition.

This is importantly different from prior parameter-sharing schemes. It is not fixed-order sharing as in ALBERT-style reuse, and it is not a standard looped/recurrent Transformer where the same ordered stack is traversed multiple times. Those methods reuse parameters but preserve each group’s relative position within a pass. Möbius Learning changes the order assignment itself. It is also distinct from pipeline parallelism, which partitions the model across a fixed stage order; here, different local streams traverse cyclically shifted orders.

The comparison baseline is well chosen: ordered-loop training uses the same loop depth and shared-parameter setup but keeps a global fixed order. That isolates the effect of cyclic ordering rather than conflating it with recurrence or parameter reuse alone.

The design rests on two implicit hypotheses. One is that gradients from shallow and deep roles do not interfere too strongly. The other is that forcing blocks to operate across multiple depth contexts may encourage more position-robust transformations. The experiments support the first claim empirically; the second remains more suggestive than fully explained.5:T57e,The main experiments use a four-worker setup with a modified GPT-2 small model at 124M parameters, trained on 2.5B FineWeb tokens with the Muon optimizer. The key comparison is against ordered-loop training under the same token budget and the same loop depth. The headline result, stated in the abstract and introduction, is that Möbius Learning achieves lower validation loss at loop depths 6, 10, and 15.

The strength of the empirical setup is the controlled comparison. The paper does not simply compare against a different architecture with different compute; it keeps the shared-depth regime and pass count aligned, then changes only whether block order is fixed or cyclically shifted across streams. That makes the evidence more directly relevant to the central hypothesis about depth-role superposition.

The current evidence is still narrow. The model is only 124M, the training budget is modest, and the system configuration is specific to four workers. The introduction emphasizes lower per-worker memory relative to replicated data parallelism, but whether this translates into better large-scale efficiency depends on details such as communication overhead, activation memory, optimizer-state footprint, and wall-clock throughput. Unless the full paper provides strong measurements there, the safe conclusion is that the method is optimization-feasible and promising, not yet system-dominant.6:T48c,SpecLA keeps the standard draft-verify-accept loop but replaces KV-cache suffix operations with mechanisms designed for recurrent states. First, topology-aware verification uses the submitted draft topology as the target-kernel schedule. Chain proposals keep tiled recurrent-state slices resident on chip, while tree proposals preserve ancestor-only dependencies through parent-pointer topologies and either tree-masked factorized GDN execution or chain-decomposed hybrid execution. Second, accepted-factor state management avoids full dense-state snapshots. The verifier already produces compact layer-specific factors sufficient to reconstruct state updates, so SpecLA buffers these factors, gathers only the accepted path after posterior selection, and applies the pending update inside the next verification kernel instead of launching a separate state-update pass. Third, target-aligned drafting treats proposal generation as cost-aware scheduling. Confidence pruning removes low-value branches before target verification, and an EAGLE-style drafter trained on recurrent target features makes proposal scores better aligned with the target model’s dynamics.7:T713,The core method is a fixed-pool diagnostic called OracleGap. For each input, a model first produces a fixed set of candidates, with the first sample treated as the reference baseline. A collaboration mechanism is then allowed only to select from that pool; it cannot repair an answer or generate a new one. This separation is deliberate. It isolates selection from repair, so gains are not conflated with the ability to create better candidates.

Within this setup, the paper defines an any-of-k oracle: if any candidate in the pool is correct under the official evaluator, the oracle succeeds. The difference between this oracle and the reference baseline is the oracle gap, which serves as an absolute upper bound for any fixed-pool selector. The realized gain is then decomposed into recoverable mass, signal coverage, conditional selection quality, and harm. Recoverable mass measures how often the baseline is wrong while at least one correct candidate exists in the pool. Coverage measures how often the mechanism produces usable evidence. Conditional quality measures how often the mechanism picks a correct candidate on recoverable examples where a signal exists. Harm measures how often the mechanism turns an already-correct baseline into an incorrect answer.

A particularly useful part of the paper is its direct measurement of verifier fidelity at the candidate level, by comparing verifier judgments against official labels. This separates poor verification from poor candidate generation. The framework is then applied to LLM selectors, generated-test verifiers, public-test verifiers, symbolic answer-equivalence selectors, and related mechanisms. The main message is that collaboration gains are constrained first by oracle gap and then by signal fidelity, not by agent count or topology alone.8:T686,The experiments cover LiveCodeBench, MATH Level-5 hard subjects, GPQA-Diamond, and HumanEval+ as a near-saturated code anchor. The paper emphasizes three-seed evaluation and hierarchical confidence intervals, which makes the comparisons more credible than single-run score reporting.

The most important findings are straightforward. On LiveCodeBench, a public-test verifier captures much more of the oracle gap because its candidate-level fidelity is high. A generated-test verifier has much lower fidelity, so even with near-zero harm its net gain is limited. This is an important distinction: low harm is not enough if the verifier cannot reliably identify correct candidates.

On MATH, a symbolic answer-equivalence selector outperforms ordinary self-consistency, while natural-language LLM selectors can be net negative. The reason is structural. Math tasks often provide normalized or symbolically checkable final answers, so a structured signal is more reliable than free-form language judgment.

On GPQA-Diamond, the candidate pool has only a 3.03 percentage-point oracle gap and high answer homogeneity. In that regime, LLM selectors are net negative. This is a useful boundary case because it shows that some tasks simply do not contain enough recoverable mass for fixed-pool collaboration to matter.

HumanEval+ serves as a code-side anchor where execution signals are strong and the task is closer to saturation, highlighting how selector behavior differs when high-fidelity external evaluation is available.

The practical rule is simple: estimate oracle gap first. If the pool contains little recoverable value, more collaboration mostly adds harm or cost.9:T506,Read this one with a diagnostic mindset. Start with the Introduction and Figure 1 to understand the distinction between fixed-pool selection and repair. Many test-time scaling papers blur that boundary; this paper is useful precisely because it does not. Then read the decomposition carefully. Recoverable mass, coverage, quality, and harm are defined on different conditional slices, and missing that point makes the later results easy to misread.

In the experiments, prioritize Figure 2 and the LiveCodeBench selector ladder, then the MATH and GPQA boundary cases. One shows how high-fidelity verification converts oracle space into real gains; the other shows why collaboration is fundamentally limited when oracle gap is tiny. The supplementary routing-eta extension is worth a quick pass because it argues that worker routing and candidate selection obey different constraints and should not be collapsed into one notion of collaboration.

As a skim paper, about 25 to 35 minutes should be enough to extract the framework and the main empirical lessons. The key thing to retain is not a specific selector score, but the decision order: first ask whether the pool contains recoverable answers, then ask whether the signal can reliably identify them, and only then discuss topology.a:T4a2,EvoGUI consists of trajectory normalization plus three VQA-style diagnostics with mechanically derived labels.
- Normalized schema: consecutive screenshots (s_t, s_{t+1}), logged action a_t (click/type/select), optional value v_t (typed text/selected option), and metadata.
- T1 Temporal ordering: given K screenshots (K=3/4/5) from the same trajectory in shuffled order, predict the chronological order.
- T2 Inverse action/value: given (s_t, s_{t+1}), predict the action class; a stricter joint variant requires exact value recovery for type/select to prevent partial credit.
- T3 One-step successor discrimination: choose the logged adjacent successor among two candidates; distractors are sampled as cross-trajectory, same-domain, or long-skip (a later state s_{t+n}, n≥3) from the same trajectory. The paper explicitly notes that since distractors are log-sampled rather than execution-validated counterfactuals, T3 measures discrimination among provided candidates, not universal impossibility.
- Quality control: acknowledges GUI-specific ambiguity (async refresh, ads, loading) and applies manual filtering/validation steps (details are in the benchmark construction section).b:T4f2,The main contribution is the Causal-Generative Dual-Judge (CGDJ), a benchmark and evaluation framework with two complementary modules. The first is Explicit Causal Perception, which tests whether a model can identify and articulate causal structure from visual evidence. Since most video generators do not support interleaved video-text prompting, the authors introduce Flatten Temporal Video: multiple frames plus rasterized queries are packed into a unified image grid, turning the task into a directly judgeable visual QA setup. This forces the model to expose what causal relation it recognizes instead of hiding behind free-form continuation.

The second module is Implicit Generative Prediction. Given a causal antecedent, the model must generate the consequent future video. This tests whether the model can enact its putative reasoning in the pixel domain over time. The key idea is to separate “knowing” from “rendering.” A model may answer causal questions correctly yet fail to generate a causally valid rollout, or it may generate something visually plausible without robust explicit causal understanding. That decomposition is more informative for world-model claims than standard fidelity metrics such as FVD or static multimodal QA benchmarks.c:T61d,The method has two layers. The first is PriVE-Bench, a counterfactual benchmark built around prior-versus-evidence conflicts. Each example contains an original image and a counterfactual image. The original conforms to common expectations, while the counterfactual violates a canonical attribute, count, part, or modality expectation. The benchmark spans five domains: object existence, counting, fashion attributes, industry/common-object attributes, and medical modality consistency. Each item defines both a visually correct answer and a prior-consistent biased answer, so model outputs can be categorized as correct, biased, or other rather than only correct or incorrect.

The second layer is PriVE-Tools. Instead of evaluating a free-form visual agent, the paper converts common visual operations into controlled evidence conditions: bounding-box overlays, crops, zoom panels, and contour overlays. The key design choice is to keep the underlying image, question, correct answer, biased answer, and scoring rubric fixed while changing only the evidence presentation. That isolates whether the evidence view itself helps the model use visual information.

The evaluation protocol includes original-only, counterfactual-only, and paired-image inputs, and supports yes/no, multiple-choice, and open-ended questions. Metrics include accuracy, prior-following error rate, and other-response rate. That metric split is important because a drop in accuracy alone cannot tell whether the model is following a prior, becoming uncertain, or producing unrelated outputs.d:T464,The main findings are clear even from the available full text. First, counterfactual inputs induce systematic prior-following errors rather than merely generic confusion. Many wrong answers land specifically on the prior-consistent biased option. Second, paired-image inputs sometimes help because they make the visual discrepancy easier to compare, but they do not reliably remove prior-following. Third, tool-derived evidence views are model-dependent: in the reported evaluations, closed-source VLMs benefit more consistently from crops and zoom panels, while several open-source models show weak or even negative aggregate effects.

The important interpretation is that the bottleneck is not only visual access. In many cases, the relevant evidence is made more explicit, yet the model still does not revise its answer. That suggests a failure in evidence weighting or decision integration, not just a failure of raw perception. The provided text does not include the full numeric tables, so it is better to avoid inventing exact percentages. The metric to watch is prior-following error rate, not just overall accuracy.e:T4f9,ATLAS implements a three-stage pipeline—Think, Plan, Paint—where layout is the cross-modal bridge.
- Shared Positional Tokens: continuous coordinates are quantized into 1000 bins; separate token sets for x and y (<pos_x_k>, <pos_y_k>) form a unified spatial vocabulary. Each object is serialized as (label, box) with four position tokens (x1,y1,x2,y2). The key design choice is that the same spatial tokens are reused across understanding, planning, generation, and editing.
- Think: a layout-aware chain-of-thought that explicitly derives entities, counts, and spatial constraints before committing to coordinates. This is meant to avoid prompt→layout shortcutting and to leverage text-side reasoning to structure the visual side.
- Plan: conditioned on the reasoning trace R, the model generates a layout sequence L = {(c_i, b_i)}.
- Paint: a Transfusion-style unified autoregressive backbone renders images from the planned layout. To improve plan-to-image fidelity, the paper adds RL-based layout alignment, optimizing generation to better match the planned boxes (mitigating missing/misaligned objects).
- Instantiation: implemented on two unified AR backbones (BAGEL and Hunyuan Image 3.0) at 7B and 80B to demonstrate scalability and architectural portability.f:T470,The method, Experiential Learning, repurposes the feedback model from an LLM-as-a-Judge into an LLM-as-a-Coach. For a prompt x, an on-policy response y, and rubrics, the feedback model produces experiential knowledge e. This e is intended to be transferable guidance for similar tasks, not an instance-specific rewrite or local critique. The experience is then placed in context for a teacher model, producing a teacher distribution conditioned on both e and x. The policy is trained on its own sampled trajectories with a token-level reverse-KL objective against this context-conditioned teacher. At inference time, neither the coach nor the experience context is needed. Compared with GRPO-style scalar reward optimization, EL gives dense per-token supervision. Compared with using raw critiques or rubrics directly, it first compresses feedback into reusable experience, which is meant to reduce overfitting to sample-specific edits. The method also supports iterative teacher updates, where a later policy checkpoint can serve as the next teacher, and it uses on-policy distillation on general-domain prompts to mitigate forgetting.10:T61e,The supplied content reports experiments across two policy families and two feedback settings: feedback from the frozen initial policy checkpoint and feedback from a proprietary API model. The evaluation covers open-ended instruction following, including held-out in-distribution prompts and several unseen benchmarks. The main finding is that EL improves over rubric-based RL and transfers better beyond the training distribution. Several ablations are described. Scalar reward optimization appears more prone to training-set reward overoptimization, while EL generalizes better. A controlled distribution-matching analysis shows that quantized scalar feedback can preserve a binary target but loses finer distinctions in a multimodal target, whereas distributional guidance recovers the target more faithfully. Distilled experiential knowledge performs better than raw critiques or rubrics. Iterative teacher updates improve task performance, and general-domain on-policy distillation reduces out-of-distribution forgetting. The provided text does not include exact model sizes, data scale, benchmark tables, or absolute score gains, so those numbers cannot be reliably reconstructed. One explicit quantitative argument is the feedback-bandwidth intuition: a 1–10 score carries at most about 3.3 bits, a bf16 reward value at most 16 representational bits, while a 1024-token experience context with a 150k-token vocabulary has an alphabet-size upper bound around 17,600 bits. The authors correctly frame this as an intuition, not a measure of usable supervision.11:T495,Read it carefully if the goal is to understand alternatives to scalar-reward post-training. Start with Figure 1 and the introduction’s information-bottleneck argument, especially the distinction between nominal feedback bandwidth and usable learning signal. Then focus on the method details: how the coach generates experiential knowledge, how the teacher is conditioned on that knowledge, and how the reverse-KL loss is estimated on on-policy samples. In the experiments, inspect the GRPO baseline setup closely: reward extraction, sampling policy, KL control, number of updates, and whether the same feedback model is used fairly. The ablations are central. The comparisons among raw critiques, rubrics, and distilled experience test whether the experience abstraction matters. Iterative teacher updates test whether the method can compound improvements. General-domain distillation tests whether forgetting is being handled rather than hidden. Two issues deserve extra scrutiny: whether the coach simply freezes judge preferences into the policy, and whether gains come from transferring a stronger teacher prior rather than from policy-specific experiential feedback.12:T43b,C-MTP is a direct-supervision method. Indirect methods, such as Coconut-style training, recurrently produce latent states and feed them back into the model. This matches inference better, but training requires sequential autoregressive passes. Prior direct-supervision methods such as CoLaR aggregate teacher CoT embeddings and ask the model to predict a latent distribution, often a Gaussian in hidden space. C-MTP takes a simpler route. It treats each latent as representing a span or step of the teacher CoT, aggregates the embeddings of the tokens in that span, and trains the model with a multi-token prediction objective over the compressed tokens. The model therefore learns latent reasoning through token likelihood rather than through explicit vector regression. This enables parallel teacher-forced training over the CoT trace. The trade-off is that the method inherits the structure and length of the teacher trace, and at inference it can suffer from accumulated errors because the model must condition on its own latent states rather than teacher-forced textual context.13:T6a8,The strongest part of the work is the experimental isolation. The authors build a frozen multi-agent pipeline in which the only changing component is the final decision-layer model. The task is a weekly up/down call on 60 Warsaw Stock Exchange equities over 18 weeks, with five samples per cell, yielding 21,600 decisions. Crucially, the task elicits no refusals: the base models complete all 10,800 base-arm decisions without refusing once. That means any delta after abliteration cannot be attributed to refusal suppression itself; it must be an off-target behavioral effect.

They also control provenance carefully: official BF16 checkpoints, one abliteration recipe, frozen prompts, and a fixed upstream pipeline. This is important because behavioral comparisons are easily confounded by quantization, checkpoint mismatch, or prompt drift. The preregistered primary endpoint is optimism rate—the share of “up” decisions under identical evidence. Additional readouts include verbalized confidence, self-justification length, and uncertainty vocabulary. A capability-covariate control is used to test whether the observed shifts are merely a byproduct of degraded instruction following.

Mechanistically, the paper does not localize the exact geometry of the edited direction inside the network. Instead, it uses behavioral readouts to falsify the “clean surgery” hypothesis. If the refusal direction were truly separable, outputs on a no-refusal task should remain stable. The observed cross-family optimism shift and lexical thinning of uncertainty language suggest that the edited direction is entangled with broader behavioral traits rather than being a strictly isolated refusal feature.14:T5bb,There are four results worth retaining. First, the preregistered main effect is a directional optimism shift that replicates across two model families: abliterated models are more likely to predict “up” under the same evidence. The abstract does not expose the exact percentage change, but the full text treats this as the central replicated finding. Second, exploratory analyses show longer self-justifications and reduced uncertainty vocabulary, indicating that the edit changes not only binary choices but also explanatory style. Third, expressed confidence does not generalize cleanly across families and even reverses sign, which is informative: the side effect is not a simple global increase in confidence but a more structured redistribution of behavioral tendencies. Fourth, the authors revisit an earlier pilot result about “excision of doubt” and show it was an artifact of contaminated provenance—a mismatched quantizer pair. Once provenance is cleaned, that earlier effect disappears or flips.

The design itself is a strength: 21,600 decisions, five repeated samples, zero generation failures, and a frozen upstream pipeline make the comparison unusually controlled for a behavioral study. The main limitation is external validity. The probe task is financially framed and binary, so the paper demonstrates off-target effects convincingly in one controlled setting, but broader generalization to other agentic tasks still needs follow-up work.15:T634,The method has two layers. The first is a deliberately simple test-time debate protocol. Multiple generator agents answer in parallel, then critic agents revise sequentially while conditioning on the other agents’ outputs. The authors are careful to frame this as cheap deliberation rather than a complex adversarial debate system.

The second layer is the main contribution: MADA-RL. Using GRPO with LoRA-based parameter-efficient tuning, the model is specialized into generator and critic roles. The key innovation is the counterfactual critic advantage. Instead of rewarding a critic simply for producing a correct final answer, the critic’s advantage is defined relative to the generator ensemble’s per-instance accuracy. In effect, the generator group provides a dynamic, role-conditioned baseline. This encourages critics to add value when generators are weak, rather than collecting reward for cases that were already easy.

Compared with standard RLHF-style or outcome-reward setups, this avoids extra machinery such as value heads, replay buffers, or external verifiers. Compared with self-refinement using a single model, it separates proposing from correcting through distinct role-specific adapters. That makes the behavioral objective cleaner and keeps training cheap.

The limits are also clear. The debate protocol itself is not the novelty; it is fairly simple. The main contribution is the advantage design plus role-specialized parameter-efficient RL. Whether the same reward construction remains robust outside automatically graded math reasoning is less certain.16:T542,The base model is DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B. The authors train a suite of LoRA agents on top of it and also evaluate several community-released 1.5B variants under a common role-specialized debate protocol. The evaluation focuses on standard math reasoning benchmarks, under both single-agent and multi-agent debate settings, with additional accounting of trainable-parameter cost and inference-token overhead.

From the full text provided, the main empirical claims are threefold. First, MADA-RL improves compact-model reasoning, and the gains are not explained solely by extra test-time deliberation; the authors include controlled analyses intended to isolate learned corrective behavior. Second, the counterfactual critic advantage provides better credit assignment than simpler reward definitions. Third, LoRA-only updates are sufficient to induce useful role specialization in a 1.5B model.

The important thing to verify while reading is the exact scale of the gains and costs: LoRA rank, fraction of trainable parameters, number of debate rounds, extra generated tokens per problem, and absolute benchmark improvements. If most of the gain comes from more inference compute rather than better-trained critics, the method becomes much less interesting. The paper claims to address this, and that analysis is central to its credibility.17:T7a9,The study uses three base Llama models: Llama-3-8B, Llama-3.2-3B, and Llama-3.2-1B. The model choice is deliberate: their tokenizers encode every integer from 0 to 999 as a single token, which avoids confounds from multi-token number representations in neuron-level analysis. The authors build equivalent arithmetic tasks in three formats: symbolic expressions, word problems, and Python code.

They then identify arithmetic heuristic neurons with a two-stage pipeline. First, attribution patching is used to find candidate layers and neurons that contribute strongly to correct arithmetic outputs. Second, activation patching is applied to test causal relevance, filtering out neurons that are merely correlated with arithmetic behavior. This is an important methodological choice: attribution alone would not distinguish true arithmetic contributors from bystanders.

The analysis then compares neuron sets across formats and performs cross-format interventions. Neurons discovered in one format are ablated or patched in another format to test whether their causal role transfers. The paper does not stop at overlap statistics. It separates shared identity from shared function, showing that a compact subset of neurons recurs across all three formats and maps onto previously reported heuristic classes such as range, modulo, pattern, identical-operands, and multi-result heuristics.

The central interpretation is that LLMs maintain a reusable arithmetic heuristic library, but different formats modulate how strongly and how reliably that library is activated. Symbolic inputs tend to trigger the circuit more directly, while text and code depend more on upstream abstraction and operand routing. Relative to earlier work, the contribution is not just “there exist arithmetic neurons,” but “the same arithmetic neurons can generalize across surface forms, and failures often come from activation-state differences rather than circuit replacement.”18:T784,The main paper focuses on Llama-3-8B, with qualitatively similar results for 3B and 1B in the appendix. The experimental findings fall into four buckets.

First, cross-format sharing. The authors identify a relatively compact set of neurons that consistently show high attribution and causal importance across symbolic, text, and code arithmetic. These neurons are concentrated in middle-to-late layers, in line with prior arithmetic-neuron studies. The exact overlap numbers are not visible in the provided excerpt, but the qualitative conclusion is clear: the shared subset is substantial enough to explain a nontrivial portion of arithmetic behavior.

Second, ablation evidence. Removing these shared neurons degrades arithmetic performance across all three formats while leaving non-arithmetic behavior comparatively less affected. This argues against the idea that they are merely format-specific lexical features. More importantly, neurons identified in one format can causally affect performance in another, which supports transfer of function across forms.

Third, activation-state analysis. On failure cases, the relevant neurons are often not absent; instead, they are weakly activated, activated at the wrong time, or combined differently with other heuristics. This is the strongest evidence for the paper’s main claim: shared circuit, different activation states.

Fourth, scale consistency. The same qualitative pattern appears in 1B, 3B, and 8B models. That makes the result less likely to be an artifact of one checkpoint or one scale regime, and suggests that form-invariant arithmetic heuristics may emerge relatively early and persist with scale.

The practical message is simple: a drop in arithmetic accuracy under reformatting does not necessarily mean the model lacks a unified arithmetic mechanism. Often the mechanism exists, but the upstream representation and triggering conditions are unstable.19:T4ab,This is a skim paper, so the efficient path is to read the Introduction for the two research questions, then the Experimental Setup to understand how equivalent symbolic/text/code inputs are constructed and why single-token numbers matter. After that, focus on the neuron-discovery pipeline. The distinction between attribution patching and activation patching is the methodological core; without it, the causal claims would be much weaker.

In the results, prioritize three kinds of figures or tables: overlap and layer distribution of neurons across formats, cross-format ablation or patching effects, and activation differences between success and failure cases. Those are enough to recover the main argument in about 30 to 45 minutes.

Two follow-up angles are worth deeper thought. One is the connection to pretraining data mixture and format balancing: if the arithmetic circuit is shared, then multi-format training may mainly improve routing into that circuit. The other is that the paper centers on MLP neurons and leaves the attention-side routing story incomplete. A natural next step is to trace how operands are extracted and delivered to these neurons across different input forms.1a:T8df,方法设计很克制。作者先构造一个 exactly labeled 的 syllogistic setting，保证 valid/invalid、satisfiable/unsatisfiable 的标签由形式逻辑直接给出，而不是人工标注。然后对冻结模型学习短 soft prefix，使其把某一类原本答对的样本推向指定目标答案。为了避免把结果误读成“prefix 学会了输出某个 token”，论文做了几层功能性解释。

第一层是区分 fixed-symbol forcing 与 semantic answer bias。作者通过随机化答案映射、统计 target-answer rate、分析对目标答案 logit/score 的直接加成，检查 prefix 是否只是强推某个表面符号。如果在答案映射变化后效果仍跟随语义而不是符号，说明更接近答案意义层面的偏置。

第二层是区分 task-specific bias 与 cross-task operation。作者分别在 validity 和 satisfiability 两个任务上训练/测试 prefix，再做跨任务迁移。如果一个 prefix 真学到更抽象的“逻辑操作”，它应在两个任务间较稳定转移；如果主要是任务内答案偏置，迁移会弱很多。结果支持后者。

第三层是看泛化边界。作者按 logical form 做 partition，在未见形式上测试；再换 wording、改 prompt interface，检查 prefix 是否只绑定训练模板。有效 prefix 在这些变化下仍能保持较高 flip，说明它不是简单记模板。

第四层是用 matched random controls 和 readable-text controls 做对照。随机前缀用于排除“任何额外连续向量都会扰动输出”的解释；文本控制用于比较可读上下文与不可读 prefix 的差异。作者还加入 reverse directions，检查这种干预是否具有明显方向性。

最后，论文用 score model 和 activation analysis 补充解释。score model 以 answer class 和 unprefixed margin 为输入，拟合加 prefix 后的 margin 变化，测试“广义答案偏置 + 原始决策边界脆弱性”能解释多少现象。activation 分析则看 learned prefix 与 norm-matched random prefix 在中间层/最终答案 margin 上造成的变化，并测试对 prefix 顺序的敏感性。整体上，这不是提出新训练法，而是把软前缀当作探针，系统拆解模型逻辑判断的脆弱来源。1b:T73e,The method is carefully structured. The authors start from an exactly labeled syllogistic benchmark where validity and satisfiability are determined by formal logic rather than annotation. They then learn a short soft prefix on top of a frozen model, targeting examples that the unprefixed model originally gets correct. The goal is to redirect those judgments toward a chosen wrong answer.

The main contribution is the functional interpretation protocol for otherwise opaque prefixes. First, the paper distinguishes fixed-symbol forcing from semantic answer bias. It randomizes answer mappings and analyzes target-answer rates and score shifts to test whether the prefix is tied to a surface token or to the meaning of an answer class.

Second, it distinguishes task-specific bias from cross-task transfer. Prefixes are trained and tested across validity and satisfiability settings. If a prefix learned something like a shared logical operation, transfer should be strong across tasks. The observed transfer is limited, which supports the answer-bias interpretation.

Third, the paper probes generalization boundaries. It evaluates on unseen logical forms, new wordings, and rephrased prompt interfaces. Strong transfer under these changes argues against simple template memorization.

Fourth, it uses matched random-prefix controls and readable-text controls. Random prefixes test whether any extra continuous context can perturb the model. Reverse-direction experiments probe whether the effect is directional rather than symmetric.

Finally, the authors fit score models using answer class and the unprefixed margin to explain prefixed behavior, and add activation analyses to compare learned prefixes against norm-matched random ones. The overall picture is that soft prefixes are used as probes for model fragility, not as a new reasoning method.1c:T5d1,The evaluation covers Qwen3.6-35B-A3B MoE, Qwen3-8B, and Gemma 4 31B. Learned prefixes flip a large fraction of previously correct judgments, and the effect survives transfer to unseen logical forms and interface changes.

On new-wording and rephrased-prompt conditions, flip rates are 72%–90% for Qwen3.6 MoE, 53%–72% for Qwen3-8B, and 40%–56% for Gemma 4 31B. Across four logical-form partitions, learned prefixes beat matched random controls in all 16 comparisons for Qwen3.6 and Gemma, with margins of 37 to 99 percentage points. One of the cleanest results is Gemma on validity: learned prefixes still achieve 54%–56% flip under wording and prompt changes, while matched random prefixes stay below 1%. The paper also strengthens the random baseline on Qwen3.6 by selecting the best prefixes from 1,000 random candidates; even then, transfer remains substantially weaker than with learned prefixes.

Mechanistically, the dominant effect is not fixed-symbol forcing but a broad preference for one answer meaning. That explains much of the aggregate steering, but not all instance-level variation. The score model fits Gemma’s prefixed margins much better than Qwen’s. For Qwen, it often predicts whether an example will cross the decision boundary, but not the final targeted margin. Activation analyses further show that learned prefixes induce much larger answer-margin changes than norm-matched random prefixes, and that sensitivity to prefix order differs across These matrices are usually trained with Adam, which treats them as a single flat vector of parameters and ignores both the matrix and product structure of LoRA. Applying a matrix-aware optimizer such as Muon to each factor does not consistently improve over Adam, and neither do the product-aware Muon variants proposed ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-20T07:16:31Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2607_17620.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL","math.OC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决 LoRA 优化器没有利用低秩乘积结构的问题。标准做法用 Adam 分别更新 A 和 B，把 LoRA 参数看成扁平向量；直接把 Muon 这类矩阵感知优化器套到两个因子上，也没有稳定收益。论文关注的是：优化器约束应当作用在有效权重更新 BA 上，而不是分别作用在 A、B 两个因子上。","importance":"这篇更偏 post-training 和参数高效微调，但对预训练优化研究有参考价值。它把 Muon 的“谱范数约束下的 steepest descent”思想迁移到 LoRA，并指出低秩因子化会改变优化几何。对基座研究员的启发有两点。第一，矩阵感知优化不能机械套用到重参数化后的变量上，优化器需要匹配模型实际看到的有效参数。第二，LoRA rank、scaling、更新幅度之间的耦合会影响调参稳定性，这对大规模 SFT、持续学习和多 adapter 训练有实际意义。","technical_approach":"PoLoRA 由三个部分组成。第一是 product-aware spectral update direction。论文先回顾 Muon：在谱范数球约束下，对线性化 loss 求最陡下降方向，解是梯度矩阵的 matrix sign，也就是 SVD 的 UV^T。对 LoRA 来说，层实际看到的是 W = W0 + BA。更新 A、B 时，一阶有效更新是 ΔB A + B ΔA，因此谱范数约束应加在这个合并更新上，而不是分别加在 ΔA 和 ΔB 上。这得到 Product Muon 类方向。第二是 curvature preconditioning。论文从控制 per-sample loss change 出发，引入曲率估计，对正交化方向做预条件，使更新不只控制层输出最大变化，也考虑样本损失局部敏感度。第三是 magnitude rule。Product Muon 主要给方向和谱范数界，但没有充分控制因子更新的大小；PoLoRA 额外设置因子更新和合并权重更新的幅度规则，避免低秩乘积的尺度自由度造成不稳定。","experiments":"实验在不同 base model、数据集和 LoRA rank 上比较 PoLoRA、Adam、直接对因子使用 Muon，以及 product-aware Muon 变体。论文报告 PoLoRA 相比调好的 Adam，达到相同 held-out loss 需要的 step 数减少 1.2–1.7 倍，每步额外开销不超过 3%。在 Llama-3.2-1B 的数学微调实验中，PoLoRA 优于若干 matrix-aware LoRA baseline。消融显示，去掉 curvature preconditioning 和 magnitude rule 后会退化到接近 Product Muon；这两个组件各自贡献了约一半的加速差距。论文还观察到 PoLoRA 的学习率更容易调：最优学习率在不同 rank 间更稳定，性能对 learning rate 的敏感性低于 Adam。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 30–45 分钟。重点看 Section 2 的推导，尤其从 Muon 的 spectral LMO 到 LoRA product-aware LMO 的变化；再看 curvature preconditioning 和 magnitude rule 的定义，它们是区别于并发 Product Muon 类工作的关键。Section 3 看主结果表和 Llama-3.2-1B math 曲线即可。Section 3.3 的消融最重要，因为它回答了 PoLoRA 的收益来自哪里。若关心大规模训练优化器，可把 Appendix B 的证明和 Appendix E 的实现细节作为补充，特别是 polar factor 近似和额外计算开销。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets an optimizer mismatch in LoRA fine-tuning. Adam treats the two LoRA factors as a flat parameter vector, while matrix-aware optimizers such as Muon applied independently to A and B do not reliably help. The effective parameter seen by the layer is the product BA, so the optimizer should constrain and scale the merged update rather than the two factors in isolation.","importance":"The contribution is mainly in post-training and parameter-efficient adaptation, but the optimization lesson is relevant to pretraining research. Reparameterization changes the geometry seen by the optimizer. A matrix-aware optimizer that works for full weight matrices may fail when the trainable variables are low-rank factors. The paper is also useful for large-scale SFT and continual adaptation, where LoRA rank, scaling, and update magnitude interact with stability and tuning cost.","technical_approach":"PoLoRA combines three ingredients. The first is a product-aware spectral direction. Muon can be viewed as constrained steepest descent under a spectral-norm ball; the linear minimization oracle returns the matrix sign of the gradient, UV^T from the reduced SVD. For LoRA, with W = W0 + BA, the first-order merged update is ΔB A + B ΔA. PoLoRA therefore applies the spectral constraint to this merged update, not separately to ΔA and ΔB. This recovers a Product-Muon-like direction. The second ingredient is curvature preconditioning derived from controlling the per-sample loss change, so the orthogonalized direction accounts for local sensitivity of the loss rather than only the worst-case layer-output change. The third ingredient is a magnitude rule that controls both factor updates and the merged weight update. This is needed because product-aware spectral directions alone do not fully determine the scale of A and B updates under the low-rank factorization.","experiments":"The experiments compare PoLoRA against tuned Adam, naive Muon on LoRA factors, and product-aware Muon variants across base models, datasets, and adapter ranks. PoLoRA reaches the final held-out loss of tuned Adam in 1.2–1.7x fewer steps, with at most 3% extra per-step overhead. On Llama-3.2-1B math fine-tuning, it outperforms the tested matrix-aware LoRA baselines. Ablations show that removing curvature preconditioning and the magnitude rule brings the method close to Product Muon; each component explains roughly half of the remaining speedup gap. The paper also reports easier learning-rate tuning: the optimal learning rate is more stable across ranks and performance is less sensitive to the learning-rate choice than Adam.","reading_guide":"Skim in 30–45 minutes. Focus on Section 2, especially the transition from Muon's spectral LMO to the product-aware LoRA LMO. The definitions of curvature preconditioning and the magnitude rule are the main technical additions beyond concurrent Product Muon-style methods. In Section 3, the key items are the main efficiency tables and the Llama-3.2-1B math curves. Section 3.3 is the most informative experimental part because it separates where the speedup comes from. For implementation-oriented readers, Appendix B and Appendix E are useful for the proofs, polar-factor approximation, and overhead details.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"做SFT/持续预训练中LoRA recipe的人重点看§3的三组件消融，可直接尝试把曲率预条件和幅值规则迁移到内部实验。","en":"For researchers working on LoRA recipes for SFT / continual pretraining, focus on §3 ablations of the three components; curvature preconditioning and magnitude rules are worth porting to internal experiments."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Flatiron Institute, Center for Computational Mathematics","Center for Computational Mathematics, Flatiron Institute"],"authors":["Nikhil Ghosh","Tetiana Parshakova","Robert M. We challenge the conventional view that these roles must remain tied to a block's position in the ordered sequence. We introduce Mobius Learning, a training architecture based on cyclic depth folding, in which different data streams follow cyclically shifted block orders. The same block group is therefore applied early in the block seque","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-20T11:37:31Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_17843.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL","cs.DC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要解决的不是“如何再堆更深的 Transformer”，而是一个更基础的问题：Transformer 的层深角色是否必须和参数位置一一绑定。标准做法里，浅层块总在前面，深层块总在后面，于是不同层逐渐形成稳定的功能分工。作者挑战这一点，提出让同一组 block 在不同数据流里既当浅层也当深层，观察这种“深度角色叠加”是否会带来优化冲突，还是反而能成为一种可行的训练组织方式。同时，这个问题还带着明确的系统动机：如果每个 worker 只存一组 block，而不是整栈模型，就能降低单卡参数内存占用。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要有两层。第一，它直接触碰了 depth specialization 这个默认假设：层的功能分化也许不是训练有效性的必要条件，至少在中小规模 GPT 预训练里，同一参数组跨浅深角色共享并不会自然崩掉。第二，它把“参数共享 / looped Transformer / distributed execution”三件事绑在一起看，而不是只把共享当压缩技巧。这个视角对后续研究有启发：如果 block 的相对顺序不是硬约束，那么深度上的参数复用、循环执行、甚至更激进的训练并行方式，都可能重新设计。贡献量级上，它更像一个新训练架构原型，而不是已经证明可大规模替代标准 stack Transformer 的 recipe。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 cyclic depth folding。模型被切成 P 个 block groups，每个 worker 持有其中一个 group。一个 batch 从哪个 worker 注入，就决定了这条数据流的“起点层”；之后 hidden states 按 worker 索引形成的环形顺序在各 group 间流动，完成若干个 loop pass。这样，同一 group 对不同数据流会处在不同的 source-relative depth：对某些样本它是浅层，对另一些样本它是深层。作者把这种现象称为 depth-role superposition。\n\n和已有方法的差别要讲清楚。它不是 ALBERT 式固定顺序共享，也不是 Universal Transformer / looped LM 那种重复同一顺序多次；后两者虽然复用参数，但每个 group 在每次 pass 里的相对位置不变。Möbius Learning 改的是“顺序本身”，不是只改“重复次数”。它也不同于 pipeline parallelism：pipeline 仍有固定 stage order，而这里不同 worker 的本地 batch 走的是循环平移后的顺序。\n\n从训练图上看，embedding 在路径起点只用一次，prediction head 在终点只用一次，中间 block groups 以循环方式串联。论文还定义了 loop depth，即完整 traversals 的次数，并用 ordered-loop training 作为对照：同样的参数共享、同样的 loop 次数，但保持全局固定顺序。这样对比比较干净，能把收益更多归因到 cyclic order，而不是单纯的共享或加深计算深度。\n\n这个设计隐含两个关键机制假设。其一，不同深度角色产生的梯度不会严重互相抵消；其二，block 可以学到更位置无关、对表示演化更稳健的变换。作者的实验结果支持“不会明显冲突”，但对第二点更多还是经验性暗示，机制解释还不充分。","experiments":"实验主体是四个 worker 上的 modded GPT-2 small，参数量 124M，训练数据是 2.5B FineWeb tokens，优化器用 Muon。主要比较对象是 ordered-loop training，在相同 token budget、相同 loop depth 下做对照。摘要和引言里给出的主结果是：在 loop depth 为 6、10、15 时，Möbius Learning 的 validation loss 低于 ordered-loop baseline。作者把这个结果作为核心证据，说明同一参数组同时承担浅层和深层角色，并没有导致可见的性能退化，反而在较大 loop depth 下更好。\n\n从论文叙述看，最重要的实验不是绝对性能，而是控制变量后的相对比较：同样 2.5B token、同样共享方式、同样 pass 数，只改 block order 是否循环平移。这个设置比拿它去和普通非共享 Transformer 比更有说服力。\n\n值得注意的是，当前证据仍有边界。规模只有 124M，训练 token 也不算大，且实验环境是四 worker 特定配置。论文在引言中强调了 per-worker memory 相对 replicated data parallelism 更低，但如果要判断它是否真能在更大模型上带来训练吞吐或扩展性优势，还需要看正文里的通信开销、bubble、activation/optimizer state 占比，以及 wall-clock 指标。如果这些部分没有充分展开，那么现阶段更稳妥的结论仍是“优化上可行且有初步收益”，而不是“系统上已经占优”。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读，但建议把问题意识读清楚。先看 Introduction 前半段，重点不是结果，而是作者如何把“层功能分化”转成一个可实验检验的训练假设。然后看 Methodology 里对 source-relative depth、cyclic starting point、loop depth 的定义，这几个概念决定了它和普通 looped Transformer 的区别。\n\n实验部分建议重点看三类图表：一是 validation loss 对比曲线，确认收益出现在哪些 loop depth；二是任何关于不同 worker / 不同起点是否均衡的分析，这关系到训练信号是否偏置；三是内存或并行执行示意，判断它的系统价值是不是实的。若时间有限，预计 20–30 分钟可抓住主线。\n\n真正值得深挖的点有两个。第一，为什么 cyclic order 在大 loop depth 下反而更好，是否因为固定顺序共享在深循环时更容易形成僵化角色分工。第二，depth-role superposition 会不会逼出更“同质化”的 block，从而影响可解释性、迁移性或下游微调方式。论文提出了现象，机制层面还留了不少空间。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a precise question: must Transformer depth roles stay tied to fixed parameter positions? In standard stacks, early blocks always operate on immature representations and late blocks always operate on already-processed states, so depth specialization emerges naturally. Möbius Learning breaks this coupling by letting the same block group appear early for some data streams and late for others. The core issue is whether such mixed shallow/deep supervision causes destructive interference, or whether depth-role sharing is a viable training regime. There is also a systems motivation: if each worker stores only one block group instead of the full stack, per-worker parameter memory drops.","importance":"Its value for pretraining research is conceptual more than immediately recipe-defining. First, it challenges the default assumption that stable depth specialization is necessary for effective LM training. At least in the reported GPT-scale setting, shared parameters can serve both shallow and deep roles without obvious optimization failure. Second, it reframes parameter sharing, recurrent depth, and distributed execution as one joint design space. That matters because many looped or shared-depth models keep the execution order fixed; this work suggests that the order itself may be a trainable degree of freedom. It first performs cross-layer semantic aggregation: the current layer hidden states form queries, while hidden states from the previous k layers, augmented with layer-index embeddings, provide keys and values. A causal mask is used so that autoregressive generation does not leak future-token information. It then adds local similarity-aware fusion over a fixed-radius token neighborhood to capture fine-grained sequence structure. Compared with methods that modify routing parameters or maintain expert-activation memory, the main change here is the construction of the router input itself. The trade-off is extra hidden-state storage and additional attention or similarity computation, which may matter in long-context and large-scale MoE training.","experiments":"The provided text reports two diagnostics: low global cross-layer consistency of expert assignments and weak local semantic coherence within sequences. We introduce L1 augmented attention, a simple and computationally parallelizable modification that subtracts a learned, head specific L1 distance between queries and keys from the dot product score. This hybrid similarity captures complementary geometric information. The useful signal is conceptual: attention logits are not a neutral semantic similarity measure, and norm effects can shape which tokens receive probability mass. This matters for studying head behavior, norm growth, logit scaling, and token-selection bias during training. However, without language-model training results, the proposal cannot yet be treated as evidence for better pretraining loss, stability, or generalization.","technical_approach":"The proposed direction is to augment dot-product attention with an L1-distance term. The paper motivates this through low-dimensional counterexamples. Dot product can rank a larger-magnitude key above a coordinate-wise closer key, and it can assign the same score to an identical vector pair and to a structurally different pair when coordinate products happen to match. L1 distance exposes coordinate-wise deviations, while dot product retains directional alignment. The intended design is therefore additive or corrective rather than a replacement of dot product. This differs from relative-position attention, feature-level attention, and KV-cache pruning methods that use norms for token importance; the target here is the query-key similarity metric itself.","experiments":"The current version mainly provides motivating examples. 里应包含对后续决策有用的行级重要性信号。\n- 探针验证：收集约 2,260 个多轮 tool responses（约 155k 行），用 Claude Sonnet 4.6 给每行打 keep/prune 标签；冻结 Qwen3-Coder-Next，把每一行的最后层 token hidden state 做 mean-pool 得到 line embedding，用 logistic 注入与行数相关的长度信息，使 head 能对短输出与长输出采取不同策略（避免一刀切）。\n  2) Per-sample balanced focal loss：按“每个样本”做 keep/prune 的平衡，而不是全局 class weight；当某个 tool output 里 keep 行很少时，提升这些稀有 keep 行的梯度权重，减少 head 学成“全 prune/偏置 prune”的风险，同时 focal 机制强调难例。\n- 部署形态：head 在 server 侧随 prefill 一起跑，复用 backbone 已算出的表示；避免额外模型调用（相对 SWE-Pruner 这类需要额外 scorer 的方案）。","experiments":"论文给出的关键数字与结论：\n- 表示可读出性（探针）：在 held-out trajectories 上，logistic regression 达到 AUC 0.83，best F1 0.63；在 keep 正例率约 30% 时，多数类 F1 上界约 0.46。\n- 端到端节省：在两种 open-weight backbones、四个 multi-turn benchmarks 上，最多节省 39% prompt+completion tokens（SWE-QA-Pro），在长上下文 Oolong 上节省 30%。\n- 质量保持/提升：在 Oolong 上 MiMo-V2-Flash accuracy +2.2；在 SWE-Bench Verified 上 MiMo-V2-Flash resolve rate +3.8%。论文还强调它是在“减少 token 的同时”保持接近 unpruned agent 的任务质量，并且在 SWE-QA family 与 Oolong generation），且不需要额外模型调用。\n（注：全文后半部分未完整给出，但从引言与动机部分已能覆盖主要实验结论与关键数字。）","reading_guide":"泛读建议（约 20–30 分钟）：\n- 先看 §2 的 probing（Figure 2）：这是论文“为什么可行”的核心证据，重点看 AUC/F1 与分布重叠区间的解释。\n- 再看 §3 方法（Figure (cat/grep/ls, etc.) in the context, which dominates token budget and exacerbates long-context degradation. Prior pruning either uses task-agnostic compression heuristics or relies on an extra scoring model plus per-turn “goal-hint” queries. This paper asks whether the pruning signal is already present in the agent backbone’s internal representations while reading tool outputs, and whether a lightweight in-server head can turn those representations into line-level keep/prune decisions without extra model calls.","importance":"Not a direct pretraining contribution (more agent/context-management), but it offers two useful signals for foundation-model researchers: (1) empirical evidence that task-relevant importance signals are decently linearly separable in frozen last-layer representations (AUC 0.83), supporting “readout from representations” as a practical design pattern; (2) a clean adaptation/deployment recipe—freeze the backbone, train a small head, reuse prefill compute—relevant to post-pretrain modular add-ons and cost control. Overall impact is mainly engineering-level token/cost reduction with moderate research novelty.","technical_approach":"SWE-Pruner Pro is an in-agent pruning head:\n- Hypothesis: since the backbone already processes tool outputs via attention, its final hidden states should encode which lines matter for the next action.\n- Probe study: collect ~2,260 multi-turn tool responses (~155k lines), label each line keep/prune using Claude Sonnet 4.6; freeze Qwen3-Coder-Next; mean-pool last-layer token states per line; fit logistic regression to test separability.\n- Model: during normal prefill, reuse the backbone’s last-layer states and run a lightweight head to output per-line keep/prune.\n- Two practical additions: (1) length-aware embedding keyed to the tool output’s line count, enabling different behavior for short vs long outputs; (2) per-sample balanced focal loss that rebalances keep/prune within each sample, preventing collapse toward pruning-dominant outputs and emphasizing rare-but-important kept lines.\n- Deployment: runs in-server on top of existing prefill, avoiding an extra scorer model call.","experiments":"Key reported results:\n- Probe: held-out AUC 0.83, best F1 0.63; gap，也就是候选池里到底有没有可捞出来的正确答案；再看验证信号的 coverage、fidelity、conditional quality 和 harm。这个框架能帮助研究者区分两类常被混淆的问题：一类是生成阶段没有产生足够多样且正确的候选，另一类是选择阶段拿不到足够可靠的判别信号。对做 RL、SFT 数据合成、或 test-time scaling 的人，这个分解很有用，因为它告诉你该把算力花在扩充候选池、改善 verifier，还是干脆别做复杂协作。","technical_approach":"方法核心是一个 fixed-pool diagnostic，名字叫 OracleGap。设模型对每个问题先生成一个固定候选池，第一条样本作为 reference baseline。后续所有 collaboration 机制都只能在这个池子里选，不能修答案、不能再生成。这样做的目的，是把 selection 和 repair 分开，避免把“会挑”与“会改”混在一起。\n\n在这个设定下，作者定义 any-of-k oracle：只要候选池里有一个答案通过官方评测，就算 oracle 成功。oracle 与 baseline 的差值就是 oracle gap，它给出任何 fixed-pool selector 的绝对上界。然后作者把实际 gain 分解成几项：recoverable mass，即 baseline 错但池中存在正确候选的样本比例；signal coverage，即 selector/verifier 在多少样本上能给出可用信号；conditional selection quality，即在 recoverable 且有信号的样本上，机制选中正确候选的概率；harm，即 baseline 本来对，但机制把它改错的概率。这个分解很干净，因为它把“有无提升空间”和“是否有能力抓住提升空间”拆开了。\n\n文章还有一个关键点是直接测 verifier fidelity。不是只看最终 task accuracy，而是看 verifier 对候选级别标签的判定，与官方 evaluator 的一致率。这样可以把“验证器不准”从“候选池本来就没好答案”里分离出来。作者据此比较了多种常见机制：LLM selector、generated-test verifier、public-test verifier、symbolic answer-equivalence selector 等。结论是，很多 collaboration 失败不是因为 agent 数量不够，而是因为信号保真度低，或者候选池本身 oracle gap 太小。","experiments":"实验覆盖四类任务：LiveCodeBench、MATH Level-5 hard subjects、GPQA-Diamond，以及 HumanEval+ 作为接近饱和的代码锚点。作者特别强调三 seed 评估和 hierarchical confidence intervals，这让结果比单次跑分更稳。\n\n最关键的结果有几条。第一，在 LiveCodeBench 上，public-test verifier 因为候选级别 fidelity 高，能捕获 oracle gap 中更大一部分可恢复收益；generated-test verifier 的 fidelity 明显更低，因此即使 harm 接近零，净收益也有限。这一点很重要：低 harm 不等于有用，如果 verifier 分不清对错，它只是“少犯错”，不是“会挑对”。\n\n第二，在 MATH 上，symbolic answer-equivalence selector 能优于普通 self-consistency，而自然语言 LLM selector 甚至可能带来负收益。原因不神秘：数学题最终答案往往可归一化或符号等价检查，结构化信号比自然语言打分更可靠。这里作者实际上是在提醒，selector 的有效性高度依赖任务是否存在高保真外部信号。\n\n第三，在 GPQA-Diamond 上，候选池 oracle gap 只有 3.03 个百分点，而且答案同质性高，LLM selector 在这种池子上是净负的。这个结果很有代表性，因为它说明有些任务不是 selector 不够聪明，而是池子里根本没多少可恢复质量，额外协作只会引入 harm。\n\n第四，HumanEval+ 被用作一个接近饱和的边界案例，说明当代码任务已有很强执行信号且候选池质量高时，selector 的空间和行为会与开放式问答明显不同。\n\n如果只记一个经验法则，就是：先估 oracle gap，再谈 collaboration；没有 gap，后面的 selector 设计大多是在噪声里做选择。","reading_guide":"这篇适合带着问题读。先看 Introduction 和 Figure 1，把 fixed-pool selection 与 repair 的边界弄清楚。很多 test-time scaling 论文把两者混在一起，这篇的价值就在于先拆开。然后看方法部分的分解公式，尤其 recoverable mass、coverage、quality、harm 各自定义在哪个条件切片上。这里如果没读清，后面很容易把 quality 误解成总体准确率。\n\n实验部分优先看 Figure 2 和 LiveCodeBench 的 selector ladder，再看 MATH 与 GPQA 两个边界案例。一个展示高 fidelity verifier 如何吃到 oracle gap，另一个展示低 oracle gap 时 collaboration 为什么天然受限。补充材料里的 routing-η 扩展可以快速翻一下，它说明 worker routing 和 candidate selection 约束不同，不该混成一个概念。\n\n作为 skim 论文，预计 25 到 35 分钟足够抓住框架和主要结论。最值得带走的不是某个具体 selector 的分数，而是这套诊断顺序：先看池子里有没有答案，再看信号能不能把答案挑出来，最后才讨论 agent 拓扑。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a basic but often skipped question: under a fixed candidate pool, when should test-time collaboration actually help, and when is extra selection or verification machinery unlikely to produce net gains? Instead of treating self-consistency, best-of-N, critics, verifiers, and multi-agent selection as inherently beneficial topologies, it reframes them as candidate-selection mechanisms whose value depends on measurable properties of the pool and the available signal.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense, but it is useful for post-training, reasoning pipelines, and evaluation methodology. Its main contribution is a clean accounting framework for collaboration gains. Before asking whether a selector is good, it asks whether there is any recoverable mass in the pool at all. Then it measures whether the verification signal has enough coverage, fidelity, conditional quality, and low harm to convert that latent opportunity into realized gain. For people working on RL, synthetic data generation, or test-time scaling, this decomposition is practical: it tells you whether to spend compute on generating a better pool, building a better verifier, or avoiding collaboration overhead entirely.","technical_approach":"$7","experiments":"$8","reading_guide":"$9","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇不改 pretrain，但会纠正你对 test-time scaling 的直觉：先看 oracle gap，再看 verifier fidelity；只读分解定义和主表就够。","en":"It will not change pretraining directly, but it fixes a common inference-time scaling misconception: check oracle gap first, then verifier fidelity; read the decomposition and main tables."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Research Institute of China Telecom"],"authors":["Jie Hu"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["test-time-collaboration","verifier","oracle-gap","signal-fidelity","evaluation"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17531","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17531","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.18232","title":"It's Not What You Say, It's How You Say It: Evaluating LLM Responses to Expressions of Belief","abstract":"Users frequently express their beliefs to large language models (LLMs). Existing evaluations are fragmented and do not systematically control for how the *form* of belief expression changes a model’s tendency to follow context. This paper builds a linguistically grounded benchmark to measure how different expressions of belief affect context-following vs prior-knowledge adherence.","importance":"Primarily an evaluation contribution. The failure mode is not missing evidence; it is accepting an answer-shaped shortcut before reconstructing the answer from lower-level records.","importance":"This is mainly an evaluation paper, but it is relevant to agent post-training and verifier design. It separates retrieval capability from evidence-use policy. A model may retrieve the right records and still fail because it stops too early or treats a secondary claim as sufficient evidence. For training research agents, this benchmark points to reward signals that should value completed evidence chains, conflict resolution, and calibrated stopping rather than only final-answer accuracy or citation count.","technical_approach":"DRNoise contains 100 tasks across 10 families of evidence operations. Each task has a paired clean and noisy corpus. In the clean corpus, the gold answer is entailed by two corroborating indirect record chains; no single document states the answer directly. In the noisy corpus, everything is held fixed except for one added document that looks ordinary and directly states a conflicting false answer. This paired intervention makes the diagnosis sharper: if an agent solves the clean task but outputs the designated false value in the noisy condition, the failure reflects how it uses evidence under conflict, not whether the task was within its basic capability. The authors analyze traces and interventions to distinguish retrieval failure, incomplete evidence-chain construction, premature stopping, and overriding of completed truthful evidence.","experiments":"The benchmark is run with the same search-agent harness across five contemporary language models. Among agents that perform well in the clean setting, adding the misleading document causes 66 to 88 percentage-point accuracy drops. The effect appears in both symbolic and numerical task families, so it cannot be reduced to arithmetic aggregation errors. Trace analysis identifies premature stopping as the dominant mechanism: agents often retrieve truth-supporting records together with the misleading document, but stop before completing and reconciling an evidence route. In fewer cases, they complete a truthful route and still submit the false value. Generic verification prompts, source-credibility changes, and retriever changes do not remove the gap. Structural changes that make truthful evidence easier to use help substantially, but the failure remains.","reading_guide":"Skim in 20 to 30 minutes. Focus first on the benchmark construction, especially the clean-noisy paired design and the requirement for two independent indirect evidence chains. Then read the main accuracy-drop results and the trace-based error decomposition. The most useful section for agent training is the intervention analysis, because it shows which mitigations are too weak and which changes improve evidence use by altering the structure of the task rather than only adding generic verification instructions.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"单条伪文档即可打穿主流 deep research agent 的事实性验证回路，这是长链推理/工具使用评测里少见的干净干预，值得读 §4 trace 分析。","en":"A single plausible fake document collapses factuality in mainstream deep-research agents; a rare clean intervention in long-horizon tool-use evaluation—read §4 trace analysis."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Hong Kong Baptist University","University of Science and Technology of China","The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology"],"authors":["Jun Nie","Zhiqin Yang","Zhenheng Tang","Yonggang Zhang","Xiaowen Chu","Xinmei Tian","Bo Han"],"notable_authors":["Bo Han"],"tags":["deep_research_agent","benchmark","misleading_evidence","verification_inertia","evaluation"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17291","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17291","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.17050","title":"EvoGUI: An Evolution-Aware Benchmark for GUI State-Transition Understanding","abstract":"GUI agents must reason about how actions transform interface states, but end-to-end success rates entangle this ability with perception, grounding, planning, and recovery. VLM/多模态预训练与 agent 方向的评测很实用：它提供了一套可迁移的、从日志自动派生标签的 transition-level probes，用来衡量模型的局部世界模型能力（forward/inverse/dynamics discrimination）。这类诊断集能反向指导数据与训练目标：如果模型在 T2（逆向动作/值）或 T3（一步可达判别）系统性失败，说明需要更强的“动作—状态”配对数据、或在预训练/中训练加入更明确的时序与交互建模信号，而不是继续堆端到端 agent rollout。","technical_approach":"EvoGUI 的核心是“轨迹规范化 + 三类 VQA 探针 + 机械派生标签”。\n- 轨迹 schema：相邻截图 (s_t, s_{t+1})，记录动作 a_t（click/type/select）、可选值 v_t（输入文本/选项），以及元数据 m_t。不同数据源（Mind2Web、WebLINX）先做 schema normalization。\n- T1 Temporal Ordering：从同一轨迹抽 K∈{3,4,5} 张截图打乱，让模型输出时间顺序，测时序结构理解。\n- T2 Inverse Action/Value：给 (s_t, s_{t+1}) 预测动作类别；并提供更严格的 joint 版本，要求对 type/select 还原精确 value，避免模型只猜动作不管内容。\n- T3 One-step Successor Discrimination：给当前状态与两个候选下一状态，让模型选日志中的真实相邻 successor。distractor 分三档：跨轨迹、同域、long-skip（同轨迹更后面的 s_{t+n}, n≥3）。作者明确：因为 distractor 来自日志采样而非执行验证的反事实，T3 测的是“在给定候选里识别相邻一步”，不是证明另一个候选在任何状态下都不可能一步到达。\n- 质量控制：作者指出 GUI 截图可能有异步刷新/广告/加载等导致视觉歧义，因此有人审/过滤流程（全文后半应给出具体规则与一致性检查）。","experiments":"EvoGUI-Bench 由 Mind2Web 与 WebLINX 构建，规模 3,000 instances，覆盖 120 domains。评测 28 个 VLM 配置的 zero-shot 表现。\n关键结果：最强模型仅 60.4 EvoGain，整体远未饱和；并分析模型规模与 GUI 相关训练对分数的影响（摘要截断处暗示 scale 与 GUI 数据/训练会显著相关）。\n从任务设计可预期的细粒度现象（通常会在全文分析里出现）：\n- T2 joint（动作+值）显著难于 action-only，暴露“读懂输入框内容/选项文本并对齐到变化”的短板。\n- T3 long-skip distractor 最具诊断性，因为它要求模型理解一步变化的局部性，而不是仅凭同域相似度匹配。\n具体分项数字与各模型配置差异需要回看主表与分任务 breakdown。","reading_guide":"泛读建议（约 25–35 分钟）：\n- 先读 whi","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-20T03:56:43Z","category_id":"eval","cover_url":"/covers/2607_17523.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要回答的问题很直接：视频生成模型看起来能“滚动未来”，但这种滚动到底是因果理解，还是高频视觉模式的拟合。作者把这个问题命名为 Thinking in Video，并进一步指出，现有评测把画面逼真度和语义逻辑拆开了，所以无法判断视频模型能不能把视频当作推理媒介。核心问题不是视频生成质量，而是 world-model 式能力是否真的存在、存在到什么程度。","importance":"对基座预训练研究的相关性主要在评测层，而不是训练方法层。它提供了一个较清楚的判断框架：如果一个模型真能作为世界模拟器，那么它既要能识别因果前提，也要能把后果在时间维度上正确生成出来。这个区分对多模态统一建模、视频 world model、AR+diffusion 联合系统都重要，因为很多系统在 demo 上看起来“懂物理”“懂社会互动”，但缺少把显式理解和隐式生成拆开测的协议。论文的价值在于把 perception-prediction gap 明确化：模型可能会生成看似合理的后续，却并不真正具备稳定的因果表征。","technical_approach":"作者提出的是一个 benchmark/evaluation framework，叫 Causal-Generative Dual-Judge，简称 CGDJ。它分成两个互补模块。第一是 Explicit Causal Perception：测试模型能否从视频中识别并表达因果结构。因为多数视频生成器不支持视频-文本交错输入，作者设计了 Flatten Temporal Video，把多帧视频和栅格化问题拼成统一图像网格，转成可直接判分的视觉问答形式。这样做的目的，是逼模型显式暴露它“看懂了什么因果关系”，而不是只靠继续生成来蒙对。第二是 Implicit Generative Prediction：给定因果前件，让模型直接生成后果视频，检查生成的时间演化是否符合因果逻辑。\n\n这个设计的关键不在 judge 本身，而在把“知道答案”和“能把答案渲染出来”拆成两个维度。作者还讨论了 audio-visual misalignment：有些模型在语言或显式判断上能说对，但在视频渲染上做不对；也有模型反过来，能生成一个看似合理的后续，但显式因果问答几乎崩掉。这样的拆分比单纯 FVD、VBench 或静态 VQA 更接近 world model 研究真正关心的能力结构。","experiments":"实验结论比较鲜明。显式因果感知这一侧，闭源强模型如 Sora-2、Veo-3.1 有可测的 causal perception，开源视频生成模型在 flatten 后的推理协议下表现接近失效。隐式生成这一侧，开源模型虽然显式理解弱，但仍能生成中等程度上“看起来合理”的因果后续，于是形成作者强调的 Perception-Prediction Gap。换句话说，视觉动作连续性可以高于显式因果理解。\n\n作者还报告了一种 audio-visual misalignment：模型有时在口头或文本层面更容易说对因果逻辑，但生成的视频并没有把这个逻辑稳定落到像素空间。这个观察对多模态预训练很有意思，因为它暗示不同模态头共享的世界知识并不充分，或者说知识到渲染的接口仍然很脆弱。\n\n从你关心的角度看，实验更像 benchmark paper，而不是机制论文。摘要没有给出详细数字，全文重点也不是追求一个统一总分，而是展示不同模型在两个子任务上的错位表现。若想把它用于研究判断，重点不是谁分高，而是“显式理解”和“隐式生成”是否同步提升。","reading_guide":"泛读建议很明确。先看 Introduction，理解作者为什么要把“Thinking in Video”从一般视频生成里单独拎出来。然后直接看 CGDJ 框架图和两个模块的任务定义，尤其是 Flatten Temporal Video 的构造方式；这是全文最有方法信息量的部分。结果部分优先看闭源与开源模型在 Explicit Causal Perception 和 Implicit Generative Prediction 上的对照图，再看作者如何定义并分析 Perception-Prediction Gap。最后浏览 audio-visual misalignment 的案例分析。预计 25 分钟左右可以抓住主线。如果你做视频 world model 或统一多模态预训练，这篇更适合作为评测设计参考，而不是训练 recipe 参考。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a clean question: when a video generator produces a plausible future, is it actually reasoning about causal structure, or merely replaying high-frequency visual regularities? The authors frame this as “Thinking in Video,” where video is treated not just as output but as a medium for constructing and verifying thought. The central issue is therefore not video quality, but whether current video generators deserve to be interpreted as world models with usable causal understanding.","importance":"Its relevance to foundation-model research is mainly evaluative rather than algorithmic. The paper offers a useful decomposition for multimodal pretraining and world-model work: a system that genuinely reasons through video should both recognize causal structure and render the correct temporal consequence. That distinction matters because many current systems look impressive in demos while existing metrics mostly reward realism, smoothness, or text alignment. By making the perception-prediction gap explicit, the paper gives a more disciplined way to discuss what video models do and do not understand.","technical_approach":"$b","experiments":"The empirical picture is fairly clear. On the explicit side, stronger closed-source systems such as Sora-2 and Veo-3.1 show measurable causal perception, while open-source video generators largely collapse under the flattened reasoning protocol. On the generative side, however, open-source models can still produce moderately plausible causal continuations. This yields the paper’s main finding: a Perception-Prediction Gap, where visual continuation can look more competent than explicit causal understanding.\n\nThe authors also report an audio-visual or verbal-visual misalignment: some models can articulate the correct causal logic more reliably than they can render it in video. That observation is interesting for multimodal pretraining because it suggests that causal knowledge, if present, is not robustly grounded into the generative visual pathway. The paper is not trying to establish a single leaderboard score; the more useful signal is the mismatch pattern between the two sub-evaluations.","reading_guide":"Read the introduction first to understand why the authors separate “Thinking in Video” from ordinary video generation. Then go straight to the CGDJ framework and the task definitions for the two modules, especially Flatten Temporal Video; that is the most methodologically informative part. In the results, focus on the side-by-side comparison between Explicit Causal Perception and Implicit Generative Prediction across closed and open models. After that, skim the case studies on the perception-prediction gap and the verbal/audio-visual misalignment. Around 25 minutes is enough for a solid skim. VLM 在“视觉证据”和“语言/类别先验”冲突时到底看不看图，进一步问的是：如果给模型更多 agentic 风格的视觉证据视角，比如框选、裁剪、放大、轮廓叠加，它会不会更愿意根据像素修正原本的先验性回答。核心问题不是再证明一次 VLM 有 prior-following，而是把这种失败模式放到一个可控的 counterfactual setting 里，检验额外证据视图是否真的能改善 grounding。","importance":"对基座预训练的直接相关性有限，因为它主要是评测资产而不是训练方法。但它对多模态预训练和 native VLM 研究仍有启发。第一，它把“看起来会答”与“是否真正依赖视觉证据”分开了，适合诊断图文对齐训练是否过度借助语言先验。第二，它提醒一个常见误区：给模型更多视觉工具或更多局部视图，不等于模型会使用这些证据。若预训练或指令微调阶段没有学会在先验冲突时重加权视觉信号，工具增强可能只增加输入复杂度。第三，PriVE-Tools 这种受控证据条件设计，对后续做多视图训练、视觉 verifier、或 tool-augmented VLM 的数据构造有参考意义。","technical_approach":"方法分两层。第一层是 PriVE-Bench，本质上是一个 prior-vs-evidence 的 counterfactual benchmark。每个样本由 original/counterfactual 图像对组成：原图符合常见视觉预期，反事实图则通过定向修改打破某个典型先验。作者覆盖五个域：object existence、counting、fashion attributes、industry/common-object attributes、medical modality consistency。每个样本都定义 visually correct answer 和 prior-consistent biased answer，因此模型输出可以被归类为 correct、biased、other，而不是只看对错。\n\n第二层是 PriVE-Tools。它不是做一个自由工具调用 agent，而是把常见视觉操作转成受控 evidence condition：bounding-box overlays、crops、zoom panels、contour overlays。关键设计是固定底层图像、问题、正确答案、偏置答案和评分规则，只改变证据呈现方式。这样能把“工具是否帮助模型利用视觉证据”从“agent 规划是否成功”“工具调用链是否稳定”等因素里剥离出来。\n\n评测协议上，作者同时测试 original-only、counterfactual-only、paired-image 三种输入模式，并覆盖 yes/no、multiple-choice、open-ended 问题形式。指标除了 accuracy，还单独统计 prior-following error rate 和 other-response rate。这个拆分很重要，因为在 counterfactual 场景里，单纯准确率下降并不能区分模型是看错了、跟随了先验，还是输出了别的无关答案。整体方法的价值在于评测控制做得比较干净。","experiments":"从全文给出的结论看，实验主要得到三点。第一，counterfactual 输入会系统性诱发 prior-following，而不只是让模型更不确定。也就是说，很多错误答案不是随机噪声，而是稳定落在“类别上通常正确、但对当前图像错误”的那个 biased answer 上。第二，paired-image 输入有时有帮助，因为原图和反事实图并排时，某些差异更容易被比较出来，但这种帮助并不稳定，也不能消除 prior-following。第三，PriVE-Tools 中的工具化证据视图效果高度依赖模型：作者评测的 closed-source VLM 在 crop 和 zoom panel 等条件下整体 grounding 有改善，而若干 open-source 模型的 aggregate 效果较弱，甚至为负。\n\n从研究解读上，这说明失败点不只是“看不到关键区域”，还包括“即便关键区域被显式暴露，模型也未必重写原有先验”。如果一个模型在 raw image 上错、在 crop/zoom 后仍错，而且错误继续落在 biased answer 上，那更像是视觉证据在决策层权重不足，而不是视觉编码器单纯分辨率不够。摘要截断没有给出完整数字，因此不宜编造具体百分点；但从作者的结论表述看，prior-following error rate 是这篇论文最该看的主指标，而不是总准确率。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读。先看 benchmark construction，确认五个 domain 的 counterfactual 是怎么做的，以及 correct / biased / other 三分类规则是否足够严格。然后看 evaluation protocol，特别是 original-only、counterfactual-only、paired-image、tool-conditioned 这几种设置如何保持其余变量不变。结果部分建议优先看不同模型在 prior-following error rate 上的变化，而不是只看 accuracy 排名；再看哪些工具条件对 closed-source 有帮助、对 open-source 无帮助，这里最能反映“证据可见 ≠ 证据被使用”。如果只想抓主线，20–30 分钟足够。更值得深挖的是数据构造细节：反事实修改是否引入低层视觉伪迹，paired-image 是否给了额外比较捷径，以及 medical modality consistency 这类域是否会把任务难度和先验强度混在一起。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a specific failure mode in VLMs: when visual evidence conflicts with learned language or category priors, do models answer from the pixels or from what is usually true? It then asks a more current question motivated by tool-augmented vision systems: if we provide extra evidence views such as crops, zooms, boxes, or contours, will the model actually revise a prior-driven answer? The contribution is not just to show that prior-following exists, but to test whether additional visual evidence helps under controlled counterfactual conditions.","importance":"Its direct relevance to pretraining is limited because this is mainly an evaluation asset, not a new training recipe. Still, it is useful for multimodal pretraining and native VLM work. First, it cleanly separates apparent task competence from genuine visual grounding. That matters when image-text pretraining may let the model rely too heavily on language-mediated priors. Second, it highlights a practical lesson: exposing more visual evidence does not guarantee that the model will use it. If pretraining or instruction tuning does not teach the model to upweight visual signals under prior conflict, tool augmentation may add complexity without fixing the decision rule. Third, the controlled evidence-view setup is a good template for future work on multi-view training, visual verifiers, or tool-conditioned multimodal data generation.","technical_approach":"$c","experiments":"$d","reading_guide":"This is a skim paper. Start with the benchmark construction and verify how the five domains are built, especially how the correct, biased, and other labels are defined. Then read the evaluation protocol to see how original-only, counterfactual-only, paired-image, and tool-conditioned settings are controlled. In the results, focus on changes in prior-following error rate rather than headline accuracy. Also pay attention to the asymmetry between closed-source and open-source models under crop and zoom conditions; that is where the paper most clearly shows that visible evidence is not the same as used evidence. A 20–30 minute read is enough for the main idea. 文本 + 连续图像 latent 的 Transfusion-style）提供了一种可落地的中间表示与训练闭环：用离散的空间位置 token 把推理链、布局计划、生成对齐串起来，并在生成阶段引入 RL 信号去对齐“计划—渲染”的一致性。这类工作对跨模态统一预训练的启发点是：1）中间结构（layout）如果能被 token 化并在多任务间共享，就能把黑盒生成变成可监督、可诊断的过程；2）RL 不只用于对话对齐，也可以作为跨模态一致性约束的后期校准信号（plan-to-image fidelity）。整体更像是“统一模型的可控生成 recipe + 评测资产（ATLAS-Reasoning）”，而不是纯算法小改。","technical_approach":"ATLAS 把生成流程拆成 Think / Plan / Paint 三段，并把 layout 提升为跨模态桥梁。\n1）Shared Positional Tokens（共享位置 token）：把连续坐标离散到 1000 桶，分别用 <pos_x_k> 与 <pos_y_k> 组成统一空间词表 V_pos。每个对象用 (label, box) 表示，box 由四个位置 token（x1,y1,x2,y2）串成序列。关键点是：同一套位置 token 同时用于理解、规划、生成、编辑，使“空间”成为统一模型内部可复用的离散接口。\n2）Think：引入 Layout-Aware CoT，让模型先在文本侧分解实体、数量、显式/隐式空间约束，再进入布局生成。这里的取舍是：不把布局当作 prompt→坐标的直接映射，而是把它当作可解释的推理产物；同时通过共享位置 token 把视觉先验（空间分布、相对位置常识）更自然地接入推理链。\n3）Plan：在推理链 R 条件下生成布局序列 L={ (c_i,b_i) }。布局是显式中间状态，可用于后续编辑与诊断。\n4）Paint：在 Transfusion-style 统一 AR backbone 上，把布局转成高保真图像 latent/像素生成。为提升“计划—渲染”一致性，引入 RL-based layout alignment：用强化学习信号直接优化生成结果与计划布局的对齐（论文动机是解决生成阶段对象偏移、漏画、错画等问题）。\n5）Backbone 与规模：在两个统一 AR MLLM backbone（BAGEL 7B、Hunyuan Image 3.0 80B）上实现，强调方法对架构与规模的可迁移性。","experiments":"论文在 7B 与 80B 两个尺度上验证可扩展性，并报告在“布局/可控生成”相关基准上优于既有 layout-based unified MLLM。引入 ATLAS-Reasoning 作为更偏“复杂空间+逻辑约束”的评测集，用来测 Think/Plan 的推理质量与生成可控性。\n从全文叙述能确认的关键实验设计点：\n- 对比对象：已有 layout-based unified MLLM（含 He et al. fidelity 的影响；(d) 7B vs 80B 的 scaling 行为。\n- 数字层面：给出的片段未包含完整表格与具体分数；但结论性描述是 80B 在生成表现上可与领先闭源大模型接近，并显著超过现有 layout-based unified MLLM。若要复现或复核，需要回到主结果表与 ATLAS-Reasoning 的分项指标（layout correctness、object
